3d:T2156,Second wave of live-proven fixes from a self-hosted mobile (LP3) deployment running this stack in production. Every change below was first shipped and measured on that live instance; this PR reconciles each against current `develop`.

## Commits

### 1. fix(shared): percent-decode command text before view-command matching
The LP3 renderer delivered `open%20notes` as literal message text. The encoded form missed the deterministic nav table and fell through to the planner: 6.3s observed live vs the sub-second fast path. A short command containing a `%XX` escape now decodes before matching; text that fails to decode (a literal `%`, "50% off") matches as written.
- Landed in `packages/shared/src/views/view-command-matcher.ts` — the single matcher source that `views-show`, the `VIEWS` action, and the `view-command-shortcut`/`view-command-routing` evaluator seam (#29320) all re-export, so every navigation path decodes consistently.

### 2. fix(core): clarifying-question REPLY terminates the planner loop in one pass
Live regression (trajectory `tj-28a877e591e5f3`): "create a mew event for today" ran 4 identical planner passes (~13.7s), each drafting the same clarifying REPLY ("What's the event for?"). The required-tool gate counted every REPLY-only pass as a miss — a clarify question is neither refusal-shaped nor widget-bearing — and the exhaustion hatch finally shipped the exact text pass 1 produced, at 4x the latency, stacking a corrective instruction into the prompt every pass (+~100 tokens/pass).
- Both required-tool miss branches now finish the loop on the pass that produced a user-safe clarifying question (explicit `messageToUser` or the REPLY call's own params text — never the native free-text fallback). Coding builds keep the full corrective budget; widget replies keep their re-emission identity check (#15230).
- `handleRequiredToolPlannerMiss` no longer appends a duplicate corrective event when the rejected draft is identical (id-insensitive) to the previous miss's.
- 6 tests, fail-first verified on the original base: one-pass termination on both planner lanes, tool-then-reply preserved, identical-draft transcript dedup, distinct drafts still recorded, coding-mode budget preserved.

### 3. feat(plugin-calendar): server-executed create-event/get-events capabilities on the calendar view
The live "create a new event for today" demo found the calendar view registering ZERO capabilities (`GET /api/views`: capabilities NONE) — no server-handled path to the calendar domain even though `CalendarService` owns real event CRUD. Following the plugin-notes precedent, the view now declares two planner-visible capabilities backed by a `serverInteract` broker that executes against the real `CalendarService` with no renderer mounted:
- `get-events`: unified-feed day/range read with honest partial/unavailable reporting.
- `create-event`: validated title/startAt/duration params writing ONLY to the built-in Eliza calendar (always writable, no provider account needed), returning an applied effect receipt bound to the durable row. Provider grants are refused with an honest pointer at the chat CALENDAR approval flow.
- Owner-gated (`roleGate minRole OWNER`) to match the owner-exclusive posture of the CALENDAR action surface.
- Device-proven live: a real event was created and read back through this path on the LP3 device.

### 4. perf(plugin-openai): warm the provider connection at message ingress
First model call after an idle gap paid ~600ms extra TTFT — DNS+TCP+TLS on the hot path: 944ms vs 345-375ms warm (raw provider floor 150ms), measured live. Preconnect the base URL via `fetch.preconnect` when a message arrives so the handshake overlaps state composition. Throttled to one warm-up per 15s; no-op where preconnect is absent; zero extra HTTP requests.

### 5. perf(agent): cache the runtime-mode snapshot on config mtime
The pre-dispatch route guard resolved the runtime mode with a full multi-file config load (parse/merge/migrate) on EVERY request. Under a zero-backoff client request storm that read amplification alone pinned the API core — observed live: ~40 config loads/sec, 130% CPU, message turns degraded to 36-93s. The snapshot is now cached keyed on the canonical config file's mtime with a 1s stat interval: a persisted mode change still applies to the next request without a restart, and steady-state requests cost one stat per second.

### 6. feat(agent): per-session request-storm cap at dispatch, self-API credential exempt
A paired-device client with a broken polling loop (no backoff, ~20 req/s sustained) pinned the API core and degraded message turns to 10-60s. Each bearer session now gets a token bucket (burst 30, sustained 10/s) answered `429 + Retry-After` before auth resolution spends anything. Bearerless requests (cookie pages) and streaming/voice/media paths are exempt.
This squashes the live saga into its proven end state: the first rollout capped the runtime's own loopback service calls (views client / app-control authenticate with the shared self-API credential), hanging turns on 429'd action calls — it was reverted live, then re-landed with the self-API credential resolved once and passed through uncapped. Regression pins 200 uncapped self-credential requests; per-device machine sessions remain capped.

## Reconciliation against develop

- **OMITTED — fix(plugin-app-control): synthesized view-interaction receipts never surface as the user reply** (live leak `tj-e721dae35d7c2f`): superseded upstream by the interact-handler rewrite in 6d67f9a914e ("synthesize structured view reads safely"), which keeps ALL capability results off the user callback (`transcriptVisibility: "internal"`, `modelReplyRequired: true`) and already pins the contract in `views-management.test.ts` ("summarizes structured interaction results without dumping JSON into chat": callback never called, no `userFacingText`, result surfaced via `data.result`). Nothing left to carry.

- **Planner clarify**: adapted to develop's `appendPlannerModelFeedbackEvent` seam (the dedup guard now also keeps duplicate corrective events out of the mirrored model history) and to coding mode being selected via the explicit `codingMode` param rather than `ELIZA_PLANNER_FULL_ACTION_SURFACE` (the coding-budget test now passes `codingMode: true`).
- **Runtime-mode cache**: adapted to develop's `loadEffectiveElizaConfig` (dev-Cloud authority view, #29342) — the cached resolver wraps the effective loader, so dev-staging linking behavior is unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Preserves well-formed Unicode across remaining preview and monogram truncation that used naive `slice(0, …)…`.

- `sidebar-auto-rail.tsx:buildRailMonogram` — `label.trim().split` + `word[0]` + `label.slice(0,1)` + `slice(0,2)` mid-surrogate → `toWellFormedUnicode` + `Array.from(word)[0]` + `Array.from(wellFormed)[0]` + `Array.from(raw).slice(0,2).join("")`.
- `ConversationsSidebar.tsx:railMonogram` — same pattern for conversation rail monogram.
- `MemoryViewerView.tsx:truncateText` — `text.slice(0, max)…` → `truncateWellFormed(toWellFormedUnicode(text), max)…`.
- `config-field.tsx:displayValue` — `raw.slice(0, 26)…` at cap 28 → `truncateWellFormed(toWellFormedUnicode(raw), 26)…`.
- `message-task-parser.ts:findTaskRegions` — `rawTitle.slice(0, MAX-1)…` → `truncateWellFormed(toWellFormedUnicode(rawTitle), MAX-1)…` plus `wellFormed` passthrough for short titles.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

<!--
Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)
Improvements (misc. changes to existing features)
Features (non-breaking change which adds functionality)
Updates (new versions of included code)
-->

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

Recent merges fixed the same class via `truncateWellFormed` / `toWellFormedUnicode` (`cockpit-modes`, `browser-session-policy`, `SaveCommandModal`, `startup-phase-poll`, `api-client`, and most recently `surrogate-label-avatar` #29550). This PR closes the remaining naive slices in `packages/ui` that are reachable with user-supplied display names, memory text, config values, and task titles containing emoji/astral characters at the truncation boundary. A lone surrogate survives to `JSON.stringify` as `\uD8xx` and is rejected by strict JSON parsers, and monogram `word[0]` returns a half for emoji-initial labels.

**Root cause:** Five UI presentation helpers used bare `slice(0, n)` for truncation/monogram without surrogate awareness (`sidebar-auto-rail.tsx:90-95`, `ConversationsSidebar.tsx:111-116`, `MemoryViewerView.tsx:232`, `config-field.tsx:74`, `message-task-parser.ts:54`).
**Affected boundary:** Any consumer rendering a rail monogram from a label starting with or containing an astral character, a memory/config/task preview whose text has an emoji straddling the 26/200 boundary, would receive ill-formed UTF-16 (`�` or lone surrogate).
**Why no higher-level test owns this:** `MemoryViewerView.test.tsx`/`ConversationsSidebar` cover loading/empty states but not surrogate well-formedness; `config-field` has no suite; `message-task-parser.test.ts` covered the 200 cap but not the surrogate boundary pre-PR. Grep for `truncateText`/`railMonogram`/`displayValue` in `*.test.*` was empty pre-PR.

40:T589,# Test coverage: origin-validation

Behavioral coverage for the shared origin validation helpers.

## Relates to

- `normalizeOrigin` / `isAllowedOrigin` in `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/security/origin-validation.ts` had no unit coverage.

## Background

These helpers normalize full URLs to origins so browser Origin headers, Referer URLs, redirect URIs, and allowlist entries can be compared safely. `isAllowedOrigin` supports exact origins, path-bearing entries, wildcard subdomains (`https://*.example.com`), and a bare `*` entry. The tests pin the normalization, wildcard (subdomain-only, not apex), and rejection branches.

## Testing

- `bun test src/lib/security/origin-validation.test.ts` from `packages/cloud/shared` → **12 pass / 0 fail / 23 expect calls**.

The module now captures `typeof process === "undefined" ? undefined : process.env` and takes the no-color path in browser contexts — the same approach as `dev-settings-banner-style` and `self-edit`. `esbuild` is declared as a shared devDependency because `theme.test.ts` imports it.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Proves the chat-first onboarding contract introduced by #25479 through the actual browser path:

- typed text is accepted by the local setup conductor before an agent exists;
- the request is retained locally and does not reach the runtime as a chat send;
- structured runtime/provider choices remain authoritative for provisioning;
- the exact request returns to the ready composer after setup for user review;
- a collapsed Cloud transcript is reopened through the real composer gesture before selecting its pending tutorial choice.

The prior smoke test only dispatched the programmatic prefill event, so it could pass without proving ordinary typed setup intent.

Bug fix: repairs stale end-to-end release contracts for a shipped onboarding path.

# Risks

Low. This removes an obsolete global coordinator lookup from the Worker client hot path after cutover has completed. Requests continue to fail closed and use the same existing `rate-limit:v2:<organization>` fixed-window Durable Object state. The server cutover endpoint and persisted state remain unchanged for compatibility and rollback.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Inference admission currently waits up to 1.5 seconds on the singleton `rate-limit:v2:cutover` Durable Object before calling the active per-organization v2 rate-only identity. If that obsolete coordinator is unavailable, otherwise healthy inference requests fail closed as `rate_limit_unavailable`.

This PR always selects the already-authoritative per-organization v2 identity for consume and prewarm. It keeps the fixed-window timestamp captured before any Durable Object queue wait, so delayed requests cannot open a duplicate window or roll durable state backward.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is needed; this retires completed internal migration coordination while preserving its server endpoint.

# Risks

Low. Changes only `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` (3 hunks): adds a 22-line helper `metadataFilterConditions` and updates two call sites (`getMemories` and `countMemories`) to use per-top-level JSONB `=` instead of `@>` containment. No schema, migration, API, or UI changes. The fix is backend-agnostic (SQL only) and matches the in-memory reference `memoryMatchesMetadata`; empty filter remains a no-op, `undefined` becomes `false` (no match). All other world-metadata logic that was previously removed in the orphan branch is now correctly preserved (the diff vs `develop` is only +55/-0 for the helper and +2 lines per call site, not the 165 deletions seen in the orphan diff).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Fixes `BaseDrizzleAdapter.getMemories` and `countMemories` to honor the documented `metadata` filter with per-top-level JSON value equality, mirroring `InMemoryDatabaseAdapter.memoryMatchesMetadata` (issue #29069).

Previously `getMemories` silently dropped the `metadata` key (no filter applied → superset returned), and `countMemories` used `metadata @> filter::jsonb` (JSONB containment). Containment recursively matches nested-object and array supersets (e.g., stored `{tags:["a","b"]}` matches filter `{tags:["a"]}`), so callers observed different results per backend. This PR makes both methods use `metadata -> key = value::jsonb` AND-combined per key, and treats `undefined` filter values as unsatisfiable (`false`) rather than dropping the key via `JSON.stringify`.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` — new `metadataFilterConditions` helper (lines ~456-485) and its two call sites in `getMemories` (≈2955) and `countMemories` (≈4525).
2. `packages/core/src/database/inMemoryAdapter.ts:138` — reference `memoryMatchesMetadata` (per-top-level equality, undefined → no match).
3. Run the focused repro: `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-sql test --run src/__tests__/unit/sql-memory-metadata.test.ts` (or the ad-hoc PGlite script below) to see that nested superset no longer matches and that `undefined` yields 0 rows.

## Detailed testing steps

```bash

# → all pass (see backend logs)

# ad-hoc PGlite proof (real DB, no mocks):
bun run plugins/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/repro-metadata-filter.mjs
# → shows:
#   stored {tags:["a","b"]} + filter {tags:["a"]} → 0 rows (correct, not 1)
#   stored {a:1,b:2} + filter {a:1} → 1 row
#   filter {x: undefined} → 0 rows (not dropped)
```

Physical iPhone pairing/TCC, real Cloud relay, VPS/SSH fingerprint validation, credentials, production deployment, signing, and release acceptance remain separate manual/external gates.4b:Ta31,## Summary

Fixes #29718. `CloudContainerService.startPolling()` schedules its async `poll` closure with `setTimeout(poll, ...)` but the closure had no try/catch around `await this.getContainer(containerId)`. `getContainer()` performs an HTTP fetch (`client.get`) that rejects on any transient error (network blip, 5xx, timeout) during the documented ~8–12 minute deployment window.

When that happened:
1. the poll promise rejected with no handler → `unhandledRejection`, which terminates the process under Node's default policy, and
2. the reject short-circuited before the next `setTimeout(poll, delay)` was scheduled → the poller stopped permanently. The container was never re-checked, never reached its tracked terminal state, and `startHealthMonitoring()` was never invoked, silently breaking the method's contract ("Poll container status until it reaches a terminal state").

The sibling `startHealthMonitoring()` uses `.then().catch()` and tolerates the same failures, so the poll path was inconsistent with the codebase's own pattern.

## Fix

`plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/services/cloud-container.ts` — `startPolling()`:

- Wrapped the `getContainer`/status handling in a `try/catch` (mirroring `startHealthMonitoring`'s tolerance).
- On a caught error: log at `warn` and **reschedule the next poll with the existing exponential backoff** (still bounded by `maxAttempts`) instead of letting the promise reject and the poller die.
- Guarded the fire-and-forget scheduling with `poll().catch(...)` so a future throw can never surface as an `unhandledRejection`.
- Backoff delay computation moved up so it is shared by the normal reschedule path and the transient-error retry path.

## Verification

Standalone Node harness (`node verify-poll-resilience.mjs`) replicating the exact patched control flow with scaled timers:

- **Transient error, then success**: `getContainer` rejects once (`ETIMEDOUT`) then returns `running` → `get()` called 2+ times, `startHealthMonitoring()` invoked after `status=running`, **0 unhandled rejections** ✅
- **Persistent errors**: `getContainer` rejects on every attempt → polling stops at exactly `maxAttempts` (120) with the timeout log, **0 unhandled rejections** ✅
- **Regression proof (old logic)**: the pre-fix closure dies after exactly 1 `get()` call and emits 1 unhandled rejection ✅ (bug reproduced, confirming the fix is what changes behavior)

The patched closure structure also type-checks standalone with `tsc --noEmit --strict`.

## Changes

- `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/services/cloud-container.ts` (+47 / −19)

4d:T912,## Problem

`loadTerminalDownloads()` loads persisted terminal-download records from `download-status.json` guarded only on `modelId` being a string and the state being terminal. A legacy or corrupt record whose `updatedAt` is missing or non-string passes the filter and poisons the in-memory terminal map. The next `snapshot()` (UI status query) or `rememberTerminalDownload()` (post-download bookkeeping) sort calls `right.updatedAt.localeCompare(...)` on that value and crashes with:

```
TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'localeCompare')
```

The sort in `rememberTerminalDownload` sits outside the J7 try/catch, so the crash propagates into the download-completion path. Same defect exists in both the plugin and the UI copy of the downloader.

## Reproduction

Write a `download-status.json` (under the `local-inference` state dir) containing a terminal job with `updatedAt` missing (or numeric), then construct `Downloader` and call `snapshot()`:

```json
{ "version": 1, "jobs": [ { "jobId": "j1", "modelId": "eliza-1-2b", "state": "failed", "error": "x" } ] }
```

→ `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'localeCompare')` on old code.

## Change

Guard the load filter the same way `modelId` is already guarded — require `typeof job.updatedAt === "string"` — in both:

- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/downloader.ts`
- `packages/ui/src/services/local-inference/downloader.ts`

Malformed records are skipped at load; the terminal map only ever holds sortable jobs.

## Testing

New regression test in `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/downloader.test.ts` feeds a status file with a missing-`updatedAt` job and a numeric-`updatedAt` job and asserts the snapshot is empty (both skipped, no crash).

- Without the fix: `1 failed — TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'localeCompare')` (verified RED by reverting the guard)
- With the fix: `29 passed (29)` — full `downloader.test.ts` suite green
- `bunx biome check` on all 3 changed files: clean

4e:T172e,# Relates to

No separate issue — the defect and its user-visible consequence are described in full below.

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

- [x] Targets `develop`, built directly on the current `origin/develop` tree, zero conflicts.
- [x] Reachability confirmed to a real HTTP route before the fix was written.
- [x] A reviewer can confirm the behaviour from the mutation output below without reading the code.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Built on the current `origin/develop` tree; zero conflicts.
- [x] Two files differ; the production change is one comparator.

# Risks

Low and strictly narrowing. Every snapshot whose `createdAt` parses keeps exactly its current position relative to other valid snapshots. Only entries that previously produced `NaN` change placement, and they move to the end instead of corrupting the ordering of everything else.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`VirtualFilesystemService.listSnapshots` sorts with:

```ts
return snapshots.sort(
  (a, b) => Date.parse(b.createdAt) - Date.parse(a.createdAt),
);
```

`createdAt` is not computed in memory — each snapshot is read back from its own `snapshot.json` on disk via `readSnapshot`. A corrupted, truncated, or hand-edited metadata file yields an unparseable string, `Date.parse` returns `NaN`, and `NaN - x` is `NaN`.

That is worse than misplacing one row. A comparator returning `NaN` is neither negative, zero, nor positive, so it violates the ordering contract: the comparison is not merely wrong but **inconsistent**, and the surviving order depends on the engine's traversal. Valid snapshots move relative to one another because of a neighbour they were never compared against meaningfully.

**Reachability.** This is not dead code — it backs a live route:

```
packages/agent/src/api/workbench-vfs-routes.ts:462
  json(res, { snapshots: (await vfs.listSnapshots()).map(snapshotView) });
```

The route serialises the array in the order returned, so a single unreadable snapshot file reorders the entire list the user sees in the workbench.

## What is the fix?

Make the comparator total: invalid entries sort last, valid entries keep newest-first.

```diff
-    return snapshots.sort(
-      (a, b) => Date.parse(b.createdAt) - Date.parse(a.createdAt),
-    );
+    return snapshots.sort((a, b) => {
+      const aCreatedAt = Date.parse(a.createdAt);
+      const bCreatedAt = Date.parse(b.createdAt);
+      if (!Number.isFinite(aCreatedAt))
+        return Number.isFinite(bCreatedAt) ? 1 : 0;
+      if (!Number.isFinite(bCreatedAt)) return -1;
+      return bCreatedAt - aCreatedAt;
+    });
```

All four cases are defined and mutually consistent — both invalid compares equal, one invalid orders after the valid one, and two valid keep the original descending comparison. `Number.isFinite` also rejects `±Infinity`, which a bare `isNaN` check would let through to produce the same degenerate ordering.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. No public surface, export, setting, or documented behaviour changes.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

The mutation block. The test drives the real `VirtualFilesystemService` against a real temporary filesystem — it creates three snapshots through `createSnapshot`, rewrites one snapshot's own `snapshot.json` with `createdAt: "not-a-date"`, then calls the real `listSnapshots`. No stubbed sorter, no re-implemented comparator in the test body.

| Gate | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/agent/src/services/virtual-filesystem.test.ts` | **9 passed** (8 pre-existing + 1 new) |
| `biome check` (repository-pinned 2.5.8) | `No fixes applied.` |
| production diff vs `develop` | one comparator |

Mutation resistance — restoring **only** the original comparator and keeping the test:

```
× sorts valid snapshots newest-first when persisted metadata has an invalid date
AssertionError: expected [ …(3) ] to deeply equal [ …(3) ]
 Tests  1 failed | 8 passed (9)
```

The two valid snapshots come back in the wrong order relative to each other — which is the point: the malformed entry does not merely land in the wrong place, it disturbs entries that are themselves perfectly valid.

# Risks

Low. This changes one isolated integration-test fixture and its test seed; no
production schema, migration, query, or runtime path changes. The main risk is
future fixture drift, bounded by running the production recovery function against
the fixture in the hosted real-PostgreSQL lane.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Restores the final durable-recovery case in the compute-stop concurrency suite.
The hand-built `agent_sandboxes` table had drifted behind the production recovery
query: it exposed only identity fields, so PostgreSQL failed the lifecycle join
with SQLSTATE `42703` before the test could evaluate recovery behavior.

The repair:

- mirrors the sandbox columns selected and filtered by
  `listRecoverableAgentComputeStopIntents`;
- seeds user/lifecycle state for the existing agent-billing concurrency case; and
- gives the terminal recovery case a matching live `dedicated-lazy` sandbox in
  due `shutdown_pending` state.

The regression is consumer-visible at the delivery boundary: it leaves
`cloud / e2e-tests` red on `develop` and removes hosted proof that terminal stop
authority remains recoverable after worker/job failure.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking, test-fixture repair).

# Documentation changes needed?

No documentation change is needed. Runtime behavior and public contracts are
unchanged; this brings the real-PostgreSQL fixture back into agreement with the
existing production contract.

# Testing

`packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/__tests__/compute-stop-concurrency.integration.test.ts`,
then compare the fixture columns with the sandbox projection and predicates in
`listRecoverableAgentComputeStopIntents`.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

It supplies the OAuth callback doubles with the collaborators required by compensation: a successful Google revoke endpoint, credential-ref adapter methods, reversible vault methods, and callback-manager storage methods. It also makes success assertions follow the staged credential ref instead of assuming the final account ID was used while staging.

The eighth failing test was separate from compensation: it still expected hostile Gmail limits to be silently normalized after the production contract had changed to reject them. The test now verifies the current fail-fast behavior and that Gmail is never called.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix to the Google Workspace test harness. No runtime behavior is changed.

5a:T16df,# Overall Project Summary: August 28, 2026

On August 28, 2026, the ElizaOS project focused on significant UI canonicalization and infrastructure hardening, including the unification of application layouts and the optimization of cloud orchestrator performance. Key technical milestones included the implementation of bulk hydration for orchestrator tasks and the restoration of native mobile runtime compatibility for both iOS and Android.

## ✅ Completed Work

**UI Canonicalization and Layout Unification**
*   Unified application layouts across Transcripts, Files, Plugins, Skills, and Runtime views ([elizaos/eliza#29598](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29598), [elizaos/eliza#29729](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29729)).
*   Simplified Wallet inventory and Projects Apps management for a cleaner user experience ([elizaos/eliza#29761](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29761), [elizaos/eliza#29741](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29741)).
*   Standardized Trajectories layout and integrated response prompts with notifications ([elizaos/eliza#29737](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29737), [elizaos/eliza#29624](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29624)).

**Cloud Infrastructure and Orchestrator Enhancements**
*   Optimized orchestrator task collections by implementing bulk hydration to replace inefficient fan-out operations ([elizaos/eliza#29685](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29685)).
*   Improved cloud reliability by retiring completed rate-limit cutover paths and failing blocked Headscale joins ([elizaos/eliza#29676](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29676), [elizaos/eliza#29687](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29687)).
*   Enhanced staging environment security and stability via revocation-fenced auth caching and provider failover bounds ([elizaos/eliza#29695](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29695), [elizaos/eliza#29692](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29692)).

**Mobile and Runtime Stability**
*   Enabled local self-hosted realtime voice and routed iOS controller claims through the native bridge ([elizaos/eliza#29633](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29633), [elizaos/eliza#29494](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29494)).
*   Restored Android native allowlist artifact-aware builds and message runtime compatibility baselines ([elizaos/eliza#29614](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29614), [elizaos/eliza#29702](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29702)).

64:Te5e,## Problem

Production Telegram turns on 2026-08-28 stopped producing a visible response after successful earlier exchanges. This is a second failure mode beside the split bot identity in #29763.

The Personal Shared internal route correctly maps SharedRuntimeCacheWarmingError to retryable HTTP 503 with Retry-After and safe X-Eliza-Failure-* headers. Later failures inside AgentRuntime are wrapped as a generic Error by run-shared-agent-turn.ts. The route therefore emits a generic 500, the direct Telegram edge retries a bounded number of times, then throws Personal Shared edge turn failed (<status>). The final provider turn receives no reply or safe fallback, and the underlying runtime/action/provider classification is lost.

The screenshots include a reminder delete request immediately before one silent interval. The existing Reminder parity issue #29688 owns the mutation behavior itself. This issue owns transport/runtime classification and the guarantee that a Telegram DM cannot end silently after an acknowledged inbound update.

## Confirmed evidence

- Four correlated production Telegram edge turns were inspected without publishing chat/user IDs or message bodies.
- The first failing class was retryable SharedRuntimeCacheWarmingError / 503.
- Later turns ended as generic AgentRuntime turn failed / 500 after bounded retries.
- The outer Worker log did not retain the safe inner cause class, so the exact downstream action/provider cause cannot be proven from current telemetry.
- Concurrent invalid API-key traffic had no trace/request intersection with the Telegram turns and is not causal.
- Railway logs in the screenshot window do not own these Telegram failures; the direct Cloudflare edge does.

## Scope

- Introduce a dependency-light, cause-preserving Shared runtime turn error with an allowlisted safe failure class and retry disposition.
- Preserve safe stage/name/retry metadata through the internal Personal Shared response, direct Telegram edge, and Railway gateway handoff without exposing provider payloads, prompts, SQL, credentials, or user content.
- Keep cache warming and transient provider/runtime availability failures retryable with bounded Retry-After semantics.
- Keep invariant/action-contract failures terminal and visible; do not replay a non-idempotent mutation after an ambiguous provider side effect.
- After retries are exhausted, deliver one truthful, non-duplicating Telegram fallback when egress is still safe, or persist a recoverable delivery state that allows a provider retry.
- Add behavioral tests for warming, transient provider failure, terminal action-contract failure, exhausted retry, exact-once fallback, and safe log/header redaction.
- Relate Reminder failures to #29688 without duplicating its parser/mutation implementation.
- No merge, deployment, live message, secret/config mutation, provider action, or webhook change is authorized here.

## Acceptance

- A safe underlying failure class and retry disposition survive AgentRuntime wrapping and both transport boundaries.
- Retryable failures remain retryable; terminal failures are not blindly replayed.
- Every accepted private Telegram update ends in exactly one of: durable normal reply, durable truthful fallback, explicit provider retry state, or a classified delivery receipt visible to operators.
- No final generic Personal Shared edge turn failed error is the only evidence available.
- Focused Shared runtime, Worker edge, gateway, and exact-once tests pass.
- A later separately authorized production/staging probe proves a normal reply and an injected safe failure path without duplicate egress.

Related: #29763, #29688, #20271, #17888.65:Tcf1,## Defect

The observed staging incident is a stale-enabled orphan reconciler, not the provision catch path. Four healthy generations with the same deterministic name were removed by direct Docker force-removal-shaped events after the reconciler five-minute grace window, despite terminal lifecycle jobs and fresh heartbeats. The deploy preserves `/opt/eliza/cloud/.env.local`, so an old `ORPHAN_RECONCILER_ENABLED=1` survives even though the daemon code defaults the feature off.

A separate code-proven latent race exists in post-create failure cleanup: the provision catch retains an exact Docker handle but discards its immutable container ID and replacement attempt when it falls back to `stopForReplacement(handle.sandboxId)`. Because the sandbox ID is the reusable container name, delayed cleanup can resolve a healthy successor.

## Production-like evidence

One staging sandbox produced four healthy same-name containers with distinct immutable Docker IDs and `ai.elizaos.replacement-attempt` labels. Docker delivered direct signal 9 events with no preceding stop/SIGTERM after each container had exceeded the five-minute grace window.

The fourth generation started at 23:57:14Z, wrote a fresh serving-row update at 00:03:51Z, then received signal 9 at 00:04:07Z and was destroyed at 00:04:08Z, about 412 seconds after start. Its database row was still `running`, carried the expected node and VPN address, and no destructive lifecycle job was active. A node-health sweep had updated at 00:03:13Z; the kill followed about 54 seconds later. The preceding generation had the same `sweep + 54 seconds -> SIGKILL` shape. This is the orphan phase signature, although the underlying read-authority mismatch that classified a live row as orphaned still requires separate investigation.

## Scope

- Make the provisioning-worker deploy overwrite stale host state with repository-owned `ORPHAN_RECONCILER_ENABLED=0`, verify it before restart, and verify it again in the health gate.
- Route failed Docker provision cleanup through its exact node, immutable container ID, replacement attempt, and VPN identity.
- Validate the expected agent/container identity and fail closed when exact Docker cleanup identity is incomplete.
- Preserve mutable sandbox-ID cleanup only for non-Docker providers.
- Do not add or mutate database schema.

## Acceptance

- A deploy rewrites a seeded host `ORPHAN_RECONCILER_ENABLED=1` to exactly one `ORPHAN_RECONCILER_ENABLED=0` entry and refuses health if it drifts.
- A stale cleanup for an old Docker ID cannot stop or remove a healthy successor that reuses the same container name.
- Cleanup still stops/removes the exact target when its immutable ID and attempt label match.
- Retry/absence stays idempotent and never targets the current name occupant.
- Focused cloud-shared and deployment reconciliation tests, typecheck, and Biome pass.

## Evidence plan

- Execute the deployment `EnvironmentFile` block verbatim against a stale seeded host file.
- Drive the real Docker identity resolver and scripted SSH transport across same-name reuse.
- Prove at service level that the provision catch passes exact handle identity and never invokes mutable-name Docker teardown.
- N/A UI, frontend, LLM, audio, and visual evidence: backend lifecycle and deployment configuration only.

# Risks

Medium. Claiming coordinates concurrent workers across 64 shards and strict tenant/node lanes. A defect could duplicate leases, starve a shard, cross an expired fence, or violate priority ordering. Durable frozen recovery cursors, trigger guards, post-lock database time, exact generation/attempt fences, and bounded `SKIP LOCKED` scans constrain those risks.

# What does this PR do?

- Claims queued `schedule_capture / reserve_capture` work with strict business-priority passes.
- Uses maximum-cardinality tenant/node lane matching without deriving authority from opaque work UUIDs.
- Adds durable, monotone recovery high-water/cursor state for deferred and expired work.
- Advances recovery and shard service turns even when raw rows are locked, preserving cross-shard fairness.
- Fences heartbeat, defer, and settlement against the exact live generation, attempt, cycle, and post-lock database clock.
- Adds real PostgreSQL and PGlite coverage, including 10,000 due rows plus 640 arrivals across all 64 shards.
- Performs database-only admission changes; capacity/autoscale/provider sentinels remain unchanged.

- Signature verification precedes payload parsing; encrypted-mode accounts cannot be downgraded to the plaintext path by request shape.

69:T18fc,# feat(core,plugin-sql): native paging for large messages and attachment text (#25140)

Closes #25140

Adds native content segmentation and paging so large message bodies and attachment text are stored as revision-fenced segments and paged on demand, instead of being written and read as single unbounded rows.

## What changes

- **`packages/core/src/memory/content-segmentation.ts` (new)** — segmentation contracts: bounded segment emission, surrogate-safe boundaries, deterministic segment identity; 14 unit tests.
- **`core memory.ts` / `types/database.ts`** — `getMemoryContentPage` paging read path; message/readAttachment actions consume pages instead of whole rows.
- **`plugin-sql` schema + store (new: `memoryTextSegments`)** — generation-addressed segments with revision fencing: `createMemory` publishes segments with a bounded marker, `updateMemory` replaces the generation and retires the stale one, large-to-small replacement retires the descriptor, deletion cascades; small content stays inline (fallback path returns null pages); large attachment text without an id or with duplicate ids is rejected.
- **Real-runtime suites (new)** — 10 real PGlite tests: parent-first publication, paging with revision fencing, reassembly across restart, atomic generation replacement, legacy large-unsegmented row flagging, owner-bound attachment paging, access-context probe coverage, rollback atomicity.

## Key invariants

- Pages are revision-fenced: a reader can never reassemble content mixing two generations.
- Generation replacement is atomic and retires the prior generation — stale segments are never served after an update.
- Legacy large unsegmented rows are flagged, not silently repaged; small rows pass through inline.

## Test-lane disclosure

Nine of the ten new plugin-sql tests live in `*.real.test.ts` files, which the PR vitest lane deliberately excludes (`plugins/plugin-sql/src/vitest.config.ts`, #10104) — they run on the post-merge lane (`VITEST_LANE=post-merge` / `TEST_LANE=post-merge`) and in the captures below. The pre-merge green checks therefore reflect the non-real suites (core unit + surface + probe tests), not the real-PGlite fencing behavior; see the backend-logs capture for the real-suite evidence. The post-merge lane does execute plugin-sql real suites.

6b:T1b29,# feat(core,plugin-sql): durable lossless manifest shard ledger with restart-safe traversal (#25141)

Closes #25141

Introduces a durable, lossless content-manifest shard ledger so progressive content (trajectories and other paginated corpora) survives restarts and concurrent writers without truncation or orphaned shards.

Investigation doc: `docs/design/context-content/25141-manifest-shard-persistence-investigation.md` (included).

## What changes

- **`packages/core/src/runtime/content-manifest-ledger.ts` (new)** — generation-addressed shard writes with a rolling `chainSha256` over shard body bytes; `ManifestHead.contentSha256` retention-bound digest (createdAt-free); load-path digest verification with a typed integrity error; deterministic idempotency (content-digest comparison on both write paths); entry-level revision containment (`entryIdentity` = kind + reference + normalized revision, range-coverage superset check).
- **`packages/core/src/types/content-manifest-shards.ts` (new)** — shard type contracts: fixed-point byteLength measure, `CONTENT_MANIFEST_SHARD_DEFAULT_ENTRIES=64`, >maxBytes typed rejection.
- **`plugin-sql/src/base.ts`** — SQL CAS split into insert-only (`onConflictDoNothing`) vs conditional-update arms (ABA-safe); loser sweeps own generation; delete/clear sweeps ledger rows (`RETURNING id`).
- **`plugin-inmemorydb` + core `inMemoryAdapter`** — atomic CAS via document mutation lock; `compareAndSwapCache` contract with base-class default for structural third-party adapters.
- **`TrajectoriesService` / agent prune paths** — prune-delete guard via `contentManifestLedgerKeys` wired into `discard` and `pruneOldTrajectories` (RETURNING-id cleanup).
- **Tests** — 48 core ledger/shard unit tests, 11 real PGlite integration + restart tests (deterministic injected-clock runs, entry-level revision containment vectors, restart superset/grown-ranges manifest assertions).

## Key invariants

- A restart never publishes a shrinking manifest that silently drops recorded entries: the restart suite asserts superset/grown-ranges (a shrinking 5-replaces-9 manifest was found by a stash control and fixed).
- Idempotency compares content digests, not wall-clock timestamps; writer races resolve CAS-safe with loser-side sweep of its own generation.
- Load-path verifies the chain digest and fails with a typed integrity error rather than serving truncated content.

6c:T1896,# Relates to

Relates to #24407. Depends on Draft #29662.

This is the repository-only enrollment producer. It does not activate a caller, claim/defer/settle work, invoke a provider, trigger autoscale, retire the legacy scheduler, or claim the 10,000-due-agent fairness acceptance.

- [x] This PR is intentionally stacked on `feat/24407-backup-admission-schema-v3` because it writes the authority introduced by #29662.
- [x] Exact base #29662 is merge-forwarded through current `origin/develop` with zero conflicts.
- [ ] Full-repository `bun run verify` was not rerun; exact affected validation is recorded below.

# Risks

Medium. Enrollment advances sandbox schedule state and creates durable backup work. A defect could skip due work, duplicate queue entries, cross a source occurrence, or strand a shard. The repository therefore uses one atomic transaction, frozen per-shard cohort authority, bounded disjoint candidate frontiers, exact source revalidation, deterministic organization locking, and replay-safe insertion.

# What does this PR do?

- Leases one of 64 durable enrollment shards with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, preferring incomplete cohorts.
- Freezes the database cutoff, snapshot xid, RPO, cohort identity, and keyset cursor for resumable enrollment.
- Selects three disjoint due frontiers, each bounded at `limit + 1`, then globally orders and caps the result.
- Revalidates active organization lifecycle and exact sandbox/node occurrence authority before mutation.
- Atomically advances `next_backup_at`, enrollment cursors, queued `schedule_capture` / `reserve_capture` work, and shard progress.
- Inserts exact replay identities with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`; queued rows begin with attempts `0`, no lease, and no claim proof.
- Performs database admission mutations only. It has no provider, provisioning, capacity, autoscale, or restore dependency.

- PGlite enrollment suite: 8/8 tests, 50 assertions. Covers bounded owner input, frozen RPO/keyset resume, two-call shard separation, source rotation, post-snapshot deferral, replay idempotence, and account lifecycle fencing.
- PostgreSQL 16 lock evidence: 1/1 test, 7 assertions. A foreign session holds shard 0; the test proves exact `55P03` before and after the repository call, bounded progress on shard 1, then shard 0 enrollment after release. It asserts two exact queued rows, attempts `0`, all lease/claim-proof fields `NULL`, and zero restore leases.

Adds `MatrixMembershipAuthority` — a publisher-discipline client for the canonical `MembershipService` contract — plus a connector-account admission gate, replacing inferred room membership with SDK-observed snapshots and ordered deltas.

## What changes

- **`src/membership.ts` (new, 886 lines)** — complete PREPARED/join roster snapshots (incomplete rosters reported incomplete, never empty), ordered join/invite/leave/ban deltas, bot self-leave scope termination, fenced retries, idempotent evidence keys.

- **`src/service.ts` (+713)** — wires SDK sync states, join/leave events, and reconnects into the authority; fresh-server roster fetch on recovery (`fetchFreshServerRoster` returns the parsed fresh roster); fail-closed on cached rosters and probe failures.
- **Tests** — 9 new recovery tests (RED control 5/6 fail pre-fix) + recovery suite; suite total 91/91 (88 at the reviewed head + 3 review-control tests added in response to the 2026-08-28 follow-up review).

## Key invariants

- Incomplete/missing rosters are reported `incomplete`/`unavailable` — never an empty roster implying mass removal.
- Cached PREPARED state (syncData.fromCache) never publishes stale evidence; publication probes persisted scopeHealth and fails closed on probe failure.
- Explicit `joinRoom` publishes the returned Room directly, guarded against self-joins beyond direct rooms.
- Unknown/missing/knock membership transitions report via `runtime.reportError('matrix:membership-transition')` and skip — never recorded as a leave.

6e:T10e3,# feat(slack): publish Web API membership snapshots and deltas (#24367)

Closes #24367

Replaces the event-only Slack membership handlers with permission-aware, account-scoped membership evidence from the Slack Web API, published to the canonical `MembershipService` contract.

## What changes

- **`src/membership.ts` (new, 233 lines)** — snapshot/delta evidence engine: `conversations.members` paging, sender renewals, join/leave deltas, multi-account identity scoping, typed error classification.
- **`src/service.ts` (+479/−55)** — wires `member_joined_channel` / `member_left_channel` / `app_mention` / reconnect events into snapshot + delta publication; partial or failed evidence degrades to `unavailable` — never an empty roster implying mass removal.
- **`src/membership.test.ts` + `src/service-membership.test.ts` (new, 645+ lines)** — deterministic permission matrices, multi-account isolation, stale-evidence-never-removes, replies-ordering regression (earliest-first per Slack docs, RED control pre-fix `[reply, parent]`).

## Key invariants

- Partial/failed/intent-missing evidence reports `unavailable`/`incomplete`; it never implies removals.
- Per-account scoping: one account's degrade never marks another account's evidence stale.
- `conversations.replies` ordering is earliest-first (parent-first) per Slack docs — the harness previously asserted the reverse, which was a production bug fixed with a RED control.

Bug fixes and design-system maintenance. No database, backend protocol, model, prompt, or deployment change.

Closes #24370

Publishes iMessage chat membership evidence to the canonical `MembershipService` contract from the native chat.db surface: committed-only inventory, observation-identity idempotency, per-chat resilient degrade, and outbound authorization gates.

## What changes

- **`membership.ts` (new)** — membership snapshot engine over chat.db: `normalizeIMessageConnectorHandle` (canonical handle index with dual-spelling keys), committed-only inventory push, prior-inventory-before-upsert with a persisted-inventory deletion ratchet on every sweep, observation-identity idempotency keys (observedAt-derived, not process counters), restart-without-chat.db degrades the persisted inventory (fail-closed), collision tombstone deny.
- **`service.ts`** — `initMembership` runs before `startPolling`; startup inventory reconcile (`reconcileRemovedScopes`) with bootstrap-failure degrade fallback; per-chat resilient degrade loop; reply callback gated (pairing); `authorizeOutboundInChat` fail-closed for governed chats with unresolvable targets; reader `rosterReadFailureCount` with adapter throwing on swallowed query failure.
- **`types.ts` / adapters** — publisher constructors take the injected normalizer; membership-adapters seam; fence-exhaustion typed error with conflict retry.
- **Tests** — 17 membership tests incl. Proxy-delegated real-authority fence-exhaustion (asserts ≥2 snapshot attempts + inventory exclusion), empty-sweep ratchet, degrade-before-persist ordering; suite total 238/238 with RED controls (3/4 fail pre-fix per round).

## Key invariants

- Restart without chat.db never implies mass removal — persisted scopes degrade to `unavailable` (fail-closed), then reconcile on recovery.
- Inventory is committed-only; an upsert never erases the prior inventory before it is read (the deletion ratchet runs every sweep).
- Outbound sends to governed chats with unresolvable targets are denied with a structured error, never silently dropped.

73:T13d7,# feat(x): publish DM membership evidence (#24372)

Closes #24372

Publishes X (Twitter) DM-scope membership evidence to the canonical `MembershipService` contract: account keyed on ownUserId, stable publisher identity with adoption, TTL-anchored own-membership renewal, and auth-degrade/restore semantics that never imply removal on transient failures.

## What changes

- **`membership.ts` (new, 890 lines)** — DM membership authority: account scoped on `ownUserId`; stable `publisherInstanceId` with adoption; own-membership published with TTL-epoch idempotency keys; own-leave revokes and degrades the scope; inviter-as-context on join evidence; all state mutations inside the serialized callback.
- **`direct-messages` wiring** — DM polling loop drives membership publication; renewal-window clears on adoption; mapping resolution inside serialization.
- **Auth recovery semantics** — 401/429 degrade the publisher durably; recovery restores BEFORE `processNewMessages` with the flag cleared only after success (`hasBoundScopes()` gate); contained failure retains the flag and retries next poll; failed own-rejoin restore withholds the roster cursor so the event reprocesses; no-bound-scopes edge clears the flag without force-restore.
- **Tests** — real-runtime PGlite suite 13/13; non-vacuous seam tests (real scope mock, publisher-call assertions, exact adoption invariant: generation=before+1 with publisher seat unchanged); RED controls per round (R4 pair proven failing pre-fix).

## Key invariants

- Degrade never publishes an empty roster: an auth failure marks the scope `unavailable` and retries; removals only ever come from explicit leave events.
- Own-account `ParticipantsJoin` after own-leave calls `restoreScope` BEFORE publishing — rejoin cannot leave the scope durably unavailable.
- Intra-epoch replay of own-proof evidence is benign (eventAnchoredAt=epoch-start on the epoch-anchored key).

74:Tc9e,## Stack / merge order

This is intentionally stacked on #29766 because both fixes touch the Telegram Worker/gateway boundaries.

1. Review and merge #29766 into `develop`.
2. Retarget this PR to `develop`.
3. Merge this PR only after its rebased checks pass.

The combined state has been conflict-resolved and validated; no fix is duplicated or dropped.

## Summary

- make the public Telegram bot identity an explicit environment contract instead of letting inbound webhook auth and outbound bot credentials drift independently
- attest the exact token against the expected bot ID and username with Telegram `getMe` before Worker or gateway traffic can reach typing, turns, delivery ledgers, reply egress, or reminders
- use the homepage bot identity as the production source of truth and a protected, fully distinct staging identity for staging
- enforce the same token, webhook-secret pair, and expected identity across Cloudflare and Railway before release
- add value-free readiness proofs and require them before deployment receipts or Personal Shared edge cutover
- cache only successful, credential-qualified attestations; failures remain fail-closed and redacted

## Verified production behavior

The read-only incident audit confirmed an identity split: onboarding sent users to the canonical public bot, while outbound replies used a stale second bot token. Direct messages to the stale bot did not reach the canonical webhook; replies appeared under the stale identity only when the canonical bot received the inbound message.

This PR makes that state undeployable and prevents either runtime from accepting or sending Telegram traffic under a mismatched identity.

No production or staging secret, webhook, deployment, provider setting, or infrastructure was changed during the audit.

## Safety

- bot ID comparison is exact; username comparison is case-insensitive and otherwise exact
- token-prefix, provider ID, or username mismatch fails before ledger, typing, turn, or egress work
- staging must be fully distinct from production
- attestation errors expose only a bounded reason and retryability, never token-bearing URLs, provider bodies, credentials, or nested causes
- deployment preflights compare protected Worker/Railway values with per-run salted digests and never print the values
- readiness responses contain only project and attested status
- reminder delivery is fenced by the same canonical identity as conversational replies

## Validation

- combined stacked state: 135 focused tests passed, 0 failed
- direct Telegram edge, gateway e2e/forwarding/groups/internal delivery, common attestation, CLI/deploy workflow contracts, and exact-once fallback coverage passed
- API, gateway, common, and Shared typechecks passed; API includes the production Wrangler dry-run
- API/common/gateway lint and `git diff --check` passed
- independent final stacked review reran 127 focused tests with 0 failures and found no blocker, conflict marker, or credential leak

The previous `Source static smoke` failure was the existing `@elizaos/ui` design-contract graph baseline (`4 !== 0` in two checks); neither this PR nor #29766 changes `packages/ui`.

Closes #29763

This PR has not been deployed or merged.
75:T9be,## Summary

- preserve bounded Shared runtime failure semantics across the Durable Object and internal HTTP boundaries
- retry Telegram turns only when the server marks the failure retryable
- deliver one exact-once, user-visible fallback for private Telegram DMs that fail before provider egress
- cover direct Cloudflare edge and Railway gateway paths, including voice resolution, transport exhaustion, malformed success payloads, and response cleanup failures
- emit allowlisted failure stage/name/cause metadata without logging provider bodies or exception messages
- keep Twilio/Blooio retry behavior unchanged and keep fallbacks out of groups and membership updates

## Verified production behavior

The read-only incident audit did not find a message-count threshold. The first silent turn failed inside Personal Shared before Telegram egress; the generic 500 then exhausted Telegram's bounded retries and produced no user-visible reply. This PR preserves the failure classification and closes every verified pre-egress path with a safe private-DM fallback.

No production data, deployment, configuration, secret, webhook, or infrastructure was changed during the audit.

## Safety

- accepted or ambiguously accepted Telegram replies are never followed by a fallback
- rejected fallback sends may retry; ambiguous provider acceptance remains tombstoned
- duplicate webhook deliveries cannot emit duplicate fallback messages
- action-contract and unknown failures fail closed instead of blindly replaying inference
- group messages and membership updates receive neither the private fallback nor typing egress
- only semantic allowlists cross runtime boundaries; raw upstream diagnostics remain inside the trusted isolate

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto `origin/develop` at `5c476b4206c18673b518afe2975a1cc06f55c8fa`; zero conflicts.

# Risks

Low. The default and scheduled paths remain non-mutating. A new manual staging-only workflow input can authorize exactly one visible Dedicated confirmation click, so an operator who deliberately enables it may start billable staging compute. The terminal evidence contract accepts only the activation/adoption and server-owned cutover POSTs attributable to that one approved click; any create, provision, delete, extra activation/adoption, or mutation without approval fails the lane. Production, scheduled, release, local, missing-approval, re-quote, cancellation, and failed-interaction paths remain fail-closed.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The credentialed Cloud browser lane previously recognized runtime, retry, and tutorial choices but not the current Dedicated adoption consent boundary. It therefore waited for the Personal identity deadline instead of reporting that explicit confirmation was required.

This PR:

- recognizes visible Dedicated adoption and activation confirmation choices during Personal identity resolution;
- reports a bounded `dedicated-confirmation-required` diagnostic without quote, selector, URL, identity, or DOM content;
- keeps the default path at zero confirmation clicks and zero adoption, activation, and cutover POSTs;
- exposes one explicit `workflow_dispatch` input only on the staging job;
- consumes that approval before interacting, so a detached control, changed quote, cancellation, or second offer cannot trigger another click;
- exercises the rendered confirmation control rather than calling an adoption endpoint or fabricating a quote ID;
- records the confirmation kind and closed lifecycle counters, allowing only the exact approved activation/adoption path plus bounded server-owned cutover polling;
- rejects every other agent lifecycle mutation in the final continuity artifact.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and live-test safety improvement.

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is required. The operator-facing workflow input describes the billable effect and defaults to disabled.

7d:T4cb3,# fix(lifeops): make sleep-cycle check-in delivery observable in scenarios (#29068)

Closes #29068

Makes check-in delivery failures observable and deterministic in scenario runs: every `DispatchResult` failure reason is mirrored in check-in status, with split status codes for unknown-recipient and envelope-type failures, and check-in report resets so failed deliveries don't mask prior evidence.

## What changes

- **`reminders-service.ts` (+112)** — unknown_recipient status split from generic delivery failure; envelope type split; `life_checkin_reports` reset (leak proven by a forced-order probe); every failure status is treated as a subsystem failure (`status !== delivered && !== skipped_already_sent`).
- **`runtime-factory.ts` (+23)** — scenario runtime exposes delivery results for assertions.
- **`lifeops/runtime.ts`, `lifeops/service.ts`, `lifeops/repository.ts`** — widen the status union to all `DispatchResult` failure reasons and preserve `delivery.reason` end to end.
- **Scenarios (new)** — `deterministic-sleep-checkin-observability` (504 lines) + `deterministic-sleep-checkin-disconnected` (276 lines), both keyless (`pr-deterministic` lane).

## Verification

| Typecheck | identical to pristine-develop control at both package scopes |
| Biome | clean |
| RED controls | r1 findings (status split, envelope split, report reset) each proven failing pre-fix |
| RP review | 3 rounds; final APPROVE zero must-fix (session ADE8A4B1) |

7e:Tb94,## Cause

The self-hosted realtime voice probe accepted weak readiness signals and was not bound to the active conversation. During a conversation handoff, a fast probe recovery could also let the realtime session restart its microphone behind an already-selected batch Talk fallback. That made text/voice ownership nondeterministic on localhost and Android kiosk/launcher flows.

## Fix

- Require an exact HTTP 200, reject redirects, require JSON media type, and accept only a ready response.
- Bind self-hosted readiness to the active conversation and require the gateway to echo that exact conversation ID. Mismatches fail closed without disclosing the configured ID.
- Keep the explicit force-only development mode unscoped and stable across conversation switches.
- Latch the current Talk session to batch ASR/TTS when conversation-scoped realtime becomes unavailable; a recovered probe cannot steal capture or playback mid-turn.
- Release the latch only after Talk is off and all batch owners are idle, so the next explicit gesture can select realtime.
- Treat probe/identity loss as an operational flag-off edge, cancelling the session hook pending identity restart before a fast recovery can reopen the microphone.
- Pass the active conversation through both mounted Shell and ChatView mint paths.

## Stack and limits

- Stacked directly on #29398 at exact head 36c467f9c4482bd0e528948260149670849f4fa7. GitHub base is fix/personal-shared-realtime-voice.
- #29398 supplies the Dedicated-only identity gate plus the Shared-startup and Personal Shared rejection regressions; this PR preserves both and adds the broader conversation-bound probe/fallback behavior.
- The incremental diff is exactly two commits: cd07df358e4 and 3e00891227c.
- The former empty-capture classification commit was dropped from this stack because develop already contains that patch through #29393; it is deliberately absent from this incremental diff.
- After #29398 lands, this PR should be retargeted to current develop before merge.
- No physical APK/device, microphone, Google sign-in, localhost browser, or live gateway mutation was performed in this tranche. Those remain explicit end-to-end proof lanes.

## Validation

- Shell plus realtime mint tests: 125/125 passed, including both inherited #29398 regressions.
- Real realtime-session client/hook tests: 37/37 passed.
- Fresh Bun gateway server harness: 1/1 passed.
- UI typecheck passed.
- Cloud API typecheck and Wrangler dry-run passed.
- Biome passed on all 8 incremental files; git diff --check passed.
- Merge-tree is clean against both exact #29398 head and current develop.

## Risk / rollback

Risk is limited to realtime-vs-batch voice ownership and the self-hosted readiness contract. The fallback remains the existing batch ASR/TTS path. Rollback requires reverting 3e00891227c and cd07df358e4 in that order. No deployment, migration, restart, container action, or live configuration change was performed.7f:T219f,# Relates to

# Risks

**Medium.** This changes failed-provision container retirement and provisioning-worker deployment configuration. Docker cleanup now fails closed when immutable identity is incomplete, which can temporarily retain a failed container for reconciliation instead of risking deletion of a healthy successor. Non-Docker cleanup keeps its existing behavior. The orphan reaper remains disabled until it has durable live-generation authority.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This contains two narrow protections:

1. The deployment workflow now owns `ORPHAN_RECONCILER_ENABLED=0`, overwrites any stale host `=1`, and verifies the value both before service restart and in the health gate. This contains the observed staging killer while its read-authority mismatch is investigated.
2. Post-adoption Docker tail failures first CAS-transfer the unchanged primary generation into the durable cleanup locator, clear every serving/runtime field, and preserve its exact container ID, replacement attempt, VPN identity, and existing capacity ownership. Only persisted cleanup may then perform remote retirement; partial provider failure remains non-serving and retryable. Mutable sandbox-ID cleanup remains available only for non-Docker providers.

There are no database or schema changes.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking lifecycle-safety and deployment-configuration change).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project-documentation change is required. The workflow is the authoritative deployment configuration, and comments explain why destructive orphan reconciliation stays disabled.

| 2 | `5986f285` | 23:33:13 | 23:38:35 | 23:38:48 | Healthy before removal; elapsed about 5m22s |
| 3 | `0dffafe3` | 23:46:12 | 23:53:57 | 23:53:59 | Recovery completed 23:46:33; fresh heartbeat 23:53:43 |
| 4 | `be3cf286` | 23:57:14 | 00:04:07 | 00:04:08 | Serving row updated 00:03:51; node-health sweep 00:03:13 |

Each generation had a distinct replacement-attempt label. Each removal was a direct signal 9 followed by destroy, with no preceding stop/SIGTERM and no active destructive lifecycle job. Generation 4 was killed about 412 seconds after start, 15 seconds after a fresh serving-row update, and 54 seconds after the node-health sweep. Generation 3 had the same sweep-plus-54-seconds shape. This timing and event shape attribute the observed incident to the stale-enabled orphan phase. The underlying authority mismatch that caused a live row to be classified as orphaned remains unresolved, so this PR explicitly disarms destructive reaping instead of claiming to repair that authority.

# Risks

Medium. This changes managed sandbox mesh admission and its provisioning-time observation. It remains fail-closed: the app still cannot start without a routed Tailscale IP. Terminal classification is limited to an explicit Headscale interactive-auth/NeedsLogin condition, the entrypoint's dedicated exit 78 marker, or a terminal container state. Generic slow/running joins remain pending for the existing registration budget.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The pinned Tailscale CLI has an unbounded default timeout. When Headscale returns a non-authorized registration that requires interactive authorization, `tailscale up --auth-key` can stay blocked forever, leaving the mesh-first entrypoint alive while the app never starts. Because the provider previously polled only Headscale registration for the full budget, retry/cleanup could not reject that candidate promptly.

This patch:

- bounds the entrypoint join with a configurable 120-second default while watching current-attempt CLI and tailscaled evidence for explicit interactive-auth/NeedsLogin states;
- terminates that blocked join promptly, redacts authorization URLs, preserves the existing rekey hook, and exits with the existing fail-closed mesh marker instead of starting the app;
- adds a cancellation-safe provider-side probe after each Headscale miss so a terminal container or explicit mesh-auth failure rejects immediately, while transport failures and ordinary running state continue polling;
- preserves success precedence: a canonical routed IP still wins before the failure probe;
- leaves the existing reusable preauth-key policy unchanged.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change is needed. The operational behavior and image/deployment path are documented below.

## Summary

Canonicalizes the `/apps` hub around the routed-page shell and a single transparent collection hierarchy. Installed apps and catalog apps now share the same compact surface, the filter and every lifecycle/catalog action remain agent-addressable, and the content rail reserves responsive clearance for the side composer.

Risk: low. The change is confined to the Apps hub presentation and its existing app-manager API calls. Catalog loading degrades independently so installed-app lifecycle controls remain available if the catalog request fails.

## Acceptance criteria

- [x] **Use the canonical page header, outer gutters, ambient canvas, and responsive composer clearance.** `/apps` keeps `FramedPage` and its canonical header/canvas, moves content into `PagePanel.ContentRail`, and applies `--eliza-chat-side-clearance` to the inline-end gutter. The post-change mobile-landscape audit reports no composer overlap.
- [x] **Present installed and available apps with one clear collection hierarchy and no decorative page-sized dark surfaces.** The former page-sized management card is replaced by peer `Installed` and `Available` collections with compact divided rows and transparent section surfaces.
- [x] **Keep app actions, status, filtering, and navigation functional and agent-addressable.** Existing create/load/relaunch/edit/stop flows remain covered; the deterministic catalog flow asserts filtering, `apps-filter`, `apps-get-Aurora Tracker`, launch payload, and route navigation.
- [x] **Prove designed-empty and deterministic populated states at mobile portrait, short landscape, tablet portrait, and desktop landscape.** The full-path Playwright lifecycle spec rendered and captured both states at `390x844`, `844x390`, `820x1180`, and `1440x900`; all four cases passed.
- [x] **The Apps hub receives no `broken` or `needs-work` audit verdict.** The focused post-change audit produced `broken=0 needs-work=0 needs-eyeball=0 good=4`.

87:T85b,## Summary

- let the deployed cloud-live certificate handle the canonical existing-Dedicated consent state during Personal identity resolution
- passively observe the quote response already requested by the renderer, compare the complete visible status, hosting, balance, runway, deficit, and state terms, then click the enabled `Confirm and continue` control
- keep the fresh-mint path unchanged and reuse the same proof helper in the deterministic onboarding browser boundary

## Regression contract

The durable staging certificate identity can legitimately have one existing Dedicated target without an adoption authority. Product UI correctly stops for explicit consent, but the cloud-live proof previously only waited for a binding, so it timed out even though the renderer showed the required consent card.

The consumer is the exact-SHA staging deployment certificate. The existing onboarding browser test owns product behavior; it did not own the deployed certificate orchestration. This change shares one browser-proof helper between both boundaries so the certificate exercises the same visible confirmation a human uses without bypassing the UI or calling the adoption API directly.

- focused full-app adoption spec setup currently remains at the existing Sign in card on current `develop` before the adoption route is reached; the isolated browser proof covers this diff and the unchanged full-app test remains wired to the same helper

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Replaces the oversized dark action container with a compact top-right `+` action and quiet overflow menu.
- Presents create and import as transparent inline forms instead of nested dark cards.
- Replaces the wide installed-app table with a responsive separator-led list and progressive action menu.
- Adds a designed empty state and keeps compact phone-landscape content clear of the composer.
- Extends real-renderer evidence across desktop, phone portrait, phone landscape, and tablet states.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and interface improvement.

# Documentation changes needed?

No product documentation change is required.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Open Projects → Apps at `/apps` and compare empty, installed, create, and overflow-menu states across desktop and mobile.

## Detailed testing steps

1. Open `/apps` and verify Apps is selected.
2. Verify `+` is the single primary action and Import is disclosed by the adjacent overflow control.
3. Open `+` and confirm the form remains on the ambient route canvas.
4. With an installed app, verify Launch remains direct and Relaunch/Edit/Stop are in the overflow menu.
5. Repeat at phone portrait and landscape widths; confirm no horizontal overflow or composer collision.

89:T499,## Summary
The Shared TODO surface renders todos without storage ids, while update/complete/cancel/delete required an opaque id — so a user-visible title/content was not a deterministic mutation handle across turns. Shared clear also executed as an immediate destructive mutation.

## Fix (#29690)
- ****: when a mutation omits `id`, resolve the target by exact visible content within the entity scope:
  - unique match → mutate that todo;
  - no match → typed `not_found` outcome;
  - duplicate content → bounded `ambiguous_match` clarification listing the matching contents.
- **update**, **complete**, **cancel**, and **delete** all fall back to the resolver when `id` is absent.
- **actionClear** now requires explicit `confirm: true`; without it, returns a bounded preview (`requiresConfirmation: true`) instead of mutating.

## Verification
Resolver classification verified independently across 4 cases: unique match (→ id), ambiguous duplicate (→ bounded clarification), no match (→ not_found), missing content (→ missing_param). All pass.

Owner/entity scope, replay keys, durable receipts, and #28015 cross-room semantics are untouched.

# Risks

Low. The change is a single classifier function (`unsubscribeMethod`) in one
plugin. It only re-orders how a `List-Unsubscribe` header is classified so the
classification matches what the executor already does. No schema, route, public
type, or export changes. The two previously-covered paths (mailto-only,
https-only) are unchanged and remain green.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Fixes a classifier/executor disagreement in `InboxUnsubscribeService`
(`plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/unsubscribe-service.ts`).

`unsubscribeMethod()` classified a sender as `"mailto"` whenever the
`List-Unsubscribe` header contained a `mailto:` entry — **even when the same
header also carried an `https://` URL and `List-Unsubscribe-Post:
List-Unsubscribe=One-Click`**. [RFC 8058 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8058#section-2)
explicitly recommends senders provide BOTH a mailto and an https URI, so this
is the common, correct-sender case.

Two contract breaks followed:

1. **Scan summary undercounted one-click eligibility** and mislabeled the method
   shown to the user as `mailto` (`oneClickEligibleCount` was 0, `mailtoOnlyCount`
   incremented).
2. **At execution `unsubscribeEmailSender` actually prefers the HTTP URL**
   (`if (sender.unsubscribeHttpUrl)` runs first) but derived `oneClick` from the
   mislabeled method, so it fired an HTTP **GET** rather than the RFC 8058
   one-click **POST** with body `List-Unsubscribe=One-Click`. Many mailer
   platforms only honor the POST, so the user is told they unsubscribed while
   the request may be a silent no-op.

The fix reorders `unsubscribeMethod()` to mirror the executor's HTTP-first
preference: when an https target is present, classify `http_one_click` (when
`List-Unsubscribe-Post` indicates one-click) else `http_get`; fall back to
`mailto` only when no https target exists; else `manual_only`. Now the summary,
the recorded `record.method`, and the request actually sent all agree.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

### Classifier/executor agreement — four header combinations

| # | `List-Unsubscribe` | `List-Unsubscribe-Post` | Classified (after) | Executor request | `record.method` |
|---|--------------------|-------------------------|--------------------|------------------|-----------------|
| a | `<mailto:unsub@brand.com>, <https://brand.com/unsub>` | `List-Unsubscribe=One-Click` | `http_one_click` | **POST** body `List-Unsubscribe=One-Click` | `http_one_click` |
| b | `<mailto:unsub@brand.com>, <https://brand.com/unsub>` | (absent) | `http_get` | GET | `http_get` |
| c | `<mailto:unsub@shop.com>` | (absent) | `mailto` | `sendMailtoUnsubscribeEmail` | `mailto` |
| d | `<https://brand.com/unsub>` | `List-Unsubscribe=One-Click` | `http_one_click` | **POST** body `List-Unsubscribe=One-Click` | `http_one_click` |

Before the fix, row (a) classified `mailto` → executor issued a **GET** (the
reported bug). Rows (c) and (d) are unchanged regression guards.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. Behavior now matches the RFC 8058 contract the package already intended; no
public API or documented behavior changed.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

It pins the complete `usePairingState` client contract and closes a real double-submit race. The hook now relies exclusively on `pairingBusyRef` for synchronous exclusion, while a focused jsdom `renderHook` suite owns status messages, error reset, input validation, in-flight state, and credential-install ordering.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix with focused regression coverage.

# Documentation changes needed?

N/A - no public API or documented workflow changed.

# Acceptance criteria

- [x] **410, 429, generic status mapping; error clears on retry.** The parameterized test asserts the exact three messages, then starts a deferred retry and observes `pairingError === null` and `pairingBusy === true` before settlement.
- [x] **Empty/whitespace input is rejected without a request.** The whitespace test asserts `Enter the pairing code from your server.` and zero `client.pair()` calls.
- [x] **Double-submit is suppressed; busy state spans the request and clears after failure.** Two submissions in one `act()` produce exactly one `client.pair()` call; busy is true in flight and false after rejection.
- [x] **Credential persistence precedes token installation.** The success test asserts `persistActiveServerCredential("paired-token", "https://runtime.example.test")`, confirms `setToken` has not run while persistence is deferred, then resolves persistence and asserts `setToken("paired-token")`.
- [x] **Measured before/after reproduction includes elapsed time, peak memory, exact command, and exit status.** Both outputs are quoted verbatim below.
- [x] **Formatting and owning-package typecheck.** Biome exited 0 with no changes; `bun run --cwd /Users/sailesh/Developer/worktrees/issue-29652/packages/ui typecheck` exited 0. Before the private install, branch and untouched-control typecheck outputs were byte-identical (871 lines, SHA-256 `1101e39d99878bd7406b4742b121fd8c005df270cd1ee63bd95c253449145d87`), confirming the dependency failures were baseline; after installing the genuinely missing dependencies, the changed worktree produced zero TypeScript errors.
